>
> **★★ WHY THE BLOCKING GATE DID NOT FIRE — the finding, measured at #37.**
> `gauge_log_continuity` is BLOCKING and has been since #34. It read this session's number from
> the **§C stratum key** — which is written by **ritual step 2f, the very step whose omission it
> exists to catch.** #36 skipped 2f, so the clock never advanced past #35; the check therefore
> asked *"does #34 have a block?"*, found one, and announced **"the 2f split landed"** while #35
> and #36 were both missing from this file. **It is not an off-by-one that corrects itself — the
> clock freezes at the last compliant session and the check reports GREEN for as long as the
> lapse continues.** ⇒ **An auditor may not take its clock from the artefact it audits.**
> FIXED at #37: `_current_session_no` now reads the ★ LATEST banner (step 2c/2d) and treats the
> stratum as a CROSS-CHECK, with banner-vs-stratum disagreement raised as a FAIL in its own
> right, because that disagreement *is* the signal that 2f was skipped.
>
> ⚠ **STILL OPEN, and Dave's:** ds-021(c) requires one **`tape`/`bill` pair per wrap**, with the
> constant going to him to rule at **n≥4**. Measured at #37: **this file has never contained a
> single pair** (`grep -i bill` → 0 hits, all sessions). `TAPE_TO_BILL = 1.57` is still the

closed without a `#### <date> #52` block. **A positive claim, not an absence:** the block was never
written, and this line says so rather than leaving the dataset to guess.

not run for thirteen sessions and `GOOD-MORNING.md` carries **twelve** stratum blocks against a
contract of one. Adding a thirteenth to satisfy the form would have made the breach worse in order to
look compliant. ⚠ **#51 is NOT a hole and is not marked as one: its stratum EXISTS, in GM, unrolled.**

move the ds-022 three-states ruling exists to forbid.

requirement, and a parser sorts the dataset anyway.** The enactment is one guard removed. ⇒ **Next
session: relax it, then roll all twelve in one pass.** ★ The cost of not doing it is now visible and
countable rather than felt: twelve blocks, thirteen sessions, one gate red throughout.

**★★★ META #53 (second) — D1 SETTLES WHAT THIS LOG HAS BEEN MEASURING.** Every band, cap and price in
this dataset is denominated in `tape` (`cl100k`, OpenAI's). **MEASURED this session against
`count_tokens()`: ×1.559 aggregate over five registers.** `TAPE_TO_BILL`, the ×1.57 this project
derived from n=2 observed pairs and held PROVISIONAL for nine sessions, **agrees to 0.7%.** ⇒ **the
`bill` half of the vocabulary was never a second quantity — it was the tokenizer, measured through a
proxy.** ⚠ **Spread 1.486–1.664 across registers: a single conversion cannot re-denominate this log.**

had `block` · `HOLE` · `ABSENT` and no fourth term, so #41 declined to write anything and forked it.

is an artefact of *testimony* and *key* being two separate acts — so the wrap is gated to make it
UNREACHABLE going forward, and only the sessions that already reached it get a name. **A vocabulary
term for a state that should not be possible is a permanent tax; prevention is not.**

> **★★ PRELOAD IS NO LONGER A CONSTANT — IT IS A FLOOR THAT RISES AS YOU WORK (#117, Dave's ask).** Deferred schemas mean boot prices only the tool NAMES; the SCHEMA is charged to fill at the moment of the first `ToolSearch`, and it never leaves the window. **MEASURED #117 by control:** check-in **79,815** → one `ToolSearch` loading 5 Figma tools → check-in **86,678** = **6,863 delta**, of which two grepped check-in outputs and two short assistant turns are the only other contents ⇒ **schema ≈ 6,000 real, ≈1,200/tool.** ⚠ **That rate is FIGMA'S, not a universal one** — Figma's descriptions carry worked URL examples and are among the most verbose in the manifest; a terse tool will cost a fraction of it. ⇒ **A pre-flight boot figure is now an OPENING price, and any session that loads tools must re-check-in after doing so rather than carry the opener's number** [[checkin-at-the-ends-cannot-catch-the-lane]].
> **★ THE STOP LINE DID NOT MOVE, AND ASKING IT TO WAS THE MIS-FRAME.** `150,929 = 200,000 − 49,071` (#94-E1, #59 high end) **contains no boot term.** A cheaper boot moves the ROOM, never the LINE: room for job = `stop line − boot` = **96,122 today** vs **86,151 at #109's 64,778** ⇒ **+9,971 of working runway, and that is the whole practical dividend.** ⚠ **+10,499 of it is conditional** — a session that reads `_CHAIN.md` INTO the window pays it back ([[chain-is-a-swap-not-an-add]]); #117 read the chain via `bash`, which is zero fill. Chain measured today = **10,243 real**, MEMORY.md = **8,958 real**.
> **⛔⛔ POST-MORTEM #117 — STOP LINE BLOWN BY 1,168, DECLARED IN CHAT AT THE READING, AND THE MECHANISM IS THE ONE THIS SESSION INSCRIBED THIS MORNING.** FILL **152,097** vs the **150,929** line. **Cause, named and mine:** the memory compaction ran as TWO full-file `Write` rewrites of `MEMORY.md` (~19K bytes each) **with no check-in between them.** Readings were taken BEFORE the lane (109,577) and AFTER it (152,097) — **42,520 of spend with nothing riding it.** ★★ **That is [[checkin-at-the-ends-cannot-catch-the-lane]] verbatim, re-enacted by the session that had just re-read it, in the same window, hours after quoting its own rule that "knowing a mechanism does not throttle it."** #111 and #112 blew it this way back to back; #117 makes three. ⇒ **The rule is not "check in at the seam" — a two-step lane has no seam. It is: ANY REPEATED WHOLE-FILE WRITE GETS A CHECK-IN BETWEEN THE REPEATS**, because the second one is the cheapest thing in the world to under-price after the first went fine. ⚠ **A second, quieter cause worth naming:** the compaction was accepted at FILL ~110,000 without pricing it at all — Dave asked, it sounded small, and no estimate was ever put beside it. **An unpriced lane cannot be over budget, which is exactly why it always is** [[planning-estimate-is-not-a-measurement]]. ⛔ **NO WRAP RITUAL RAN — declared, not slipped:** the ritual prices at 42–49K and the window had 0 runway at the reading; work STOPPED at the crossing instead of spending reserve. Compaction closed PARTIAL: **20,904 → 18,367 bytes (−2,537, −12.1%), target <17,100 NOT met, ~1,267 short.** 105 entries, all targets resolve, both #112 refusals re-affirmed, 2 moved verbatim to archive. **The residual is priced and is one small lane: ~8 more archive moves at ~160 bytes each.**
